任务要求：

1. 检测任何差异
   把官方模板和我们当前论文用的 template 做完整 diff，任何差异都不能漏掉，包括但不限于：
   - 文档类参数：\documentclass[...]{acl} 的所有选项是否一致

105	\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}
106	\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}
107	\label{sec:diagnostics}
108	
109	\looseness=-1 Three lightweight diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run (Figure~\ref{fig:diagnostic-flowchart}): (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\geq 60\%$, BR $> 1$ $\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\leq 40\%$ $\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.
110	
111	\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\Delta = -4.41$\,pp) and non-significance ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).
112	
113	\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}
114	\label{sec:cross-arch}
```

25	\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\kappa = 0.15$, $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\kappa$ (${\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\ref{app:intervention-individual}).

101	\caption{Diagnostic decision flowchart. Three lightweight metrics from a $K{=}12$ pilot run predict a priori whether self-extraction aggregation will improve over SC: degenerate-regime indicator ($C_0$), diversity floor ($K_{\text{eff}}$), and bias--regime interaction (BR $+$ SC level). All 60 tested conditions are correctly classified.}
102	\label{fig:diagnostic-flowchart}
103	\end{figure}
104	
105	\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}
106	\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}
107	\label{sec:diagnostics}
108	
109	\looseness=-1 Three lightweight diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run (Figure~\ref{fig:diagnostic-flowchart}): (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\geq 60\%$, BR $> 1$ $\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\leq 40\%$ $\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (three primary generators plus six additional models; BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral (constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups), and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}(A,C)$ generation structure provides empirical motivation \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration (Observation~1), constraints carry no more task-relevant information than answers alone, i.e., $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three lightweight diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run (Figure~\\ref{fig:diagnostic-flowchart}): (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for natural language inference (NLI). Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}); corrective signal must therefore originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does; structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n\n",

````text
Now let me do a systematic comparison of the `main.tex` preamble against the official `acl_latex.tex` preamble. I've already read both. Let me compile the analysis.

Here is the complete comparison report:

---

## (a) `acl.sty` -- Our file vs official

**Identical.** MD5 checksums match: `e2c027f483aa40e577ed34264c8b0655`. Zero differences, byte-for-byte identical to the latest version on `github.com/acl-org/acl-style-files` (master branch, commit `2353f3e`).

---

## (b) `acl_natbib.bst` -- Our file vs official

**Identical.** MD5 checksums match: `3d1af46d62cc9172ef6e42b56c1cd613`. Zero differences.

---

## (c) `main.tex` preamble vs official `acl_latex.tex` preamble

The official sample preamble is:
```latex
\documentclass[11pt]{article}
\usepackage[review]{acl}
\usepackage{times}
\usepackage{latexsym}
\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}
\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}
\usepackage{microtype}
\usepackage{inconsolata}
\usepackage{graphicx}
```

**Matching elements** (present in both):
- `\documentclass[11pt]{article}` -- identical
- `\usepackage[review]{acl}` -- identical
- `\usepackage{times}` -- identical
- `\usepackage{latexsym}` -- identical
- `\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}` -- identical
- `\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}` -- identical
- `\usepackage{microtype}` -- identical
- `\usepackage{graphicx}` -- identical

**Differences:**

1. **`inconsolata` package -- REMOVED in ours** (line 8 has comment: `% \usepackage{inconsolata} % removed: not available in this TeX distribution`). The official template includes it for better typewriter font aesthetics. This is cosmetic and the official template itself says "may be commented out." **Low risk** -- only affects `\texttt{}` rendering slightly.

2. **`hyperref` loaded explicitly in our preamble** (line 25: `\usepackage{hyperref}`). The official template does NOT include this because `acl.sty` already loads `hyperref` internally (via `\RequirePackage{hyperref}` at line 302 of `acl.sty`). Loading it twice should be harmless (LaTeX silently ignores duplicate `\usepackage` calls), but it is redundant. **No risk** -- but unnecessary.

---

## (d) Extra packages we added (not in official sample)

These are content packages we added for our paper. None of them modify the ACL formatting:

| Package | Purpose | Risk |
|---------|---------|------|
| `amsmath` | Math environments | Safe |
| `amssymb` | Math symbols | Safe |
| `amsthm` | Theorem environments | Safe |
| `booktabs` | Better table rules | Safe |
| `multirow` | Multi-row table cells | Safe |
| `xcolor` | Colors (already loaded by `acl.sty`) | Redundant but safe |
| `enumitem` | Customized lists | Safe, but **could theoretically change list spacing** from ACL defaults if used with options that override the spacing set in `acl.sty`. Verify no global `\setlist` overrides are used. |
| `pifont` | Ding symbols | Safe |
| `subcaption` | Sub-figures | Safe (compatible with `caption` loaded by `acl.sty`) |
| `tikz` + libraries | Diagrams | Safe |
| `listings` | Code listings | Safe |
| `algorithm` + `algpseudocode` | Pseudocode | Safe |

**One additional concern**: Lines 51-59 of `main.tex` override various spacing parameters (`\textfloatsep`, `\floatsep`, `\abovecaptionskip`, `\belowcaptionskip`, `\abovedisplayskip`, `\belowdisplayskip`, `\intextsep`) to save space. These are **not** in the official template. They tighten float/display spacing beyond ACL defaults. This is a common practice and reviewers rarely flag it, but it does deviate from the official layout defaults defined in `acl.sty`.

---

## Summary

- **`acl.sty`**: Byte-identical to official. No concerns.
- **`acl_natbib.bst`**: Byte-identical to official. No concerns.
- **`main.tex` preamble vs official**:
  - `inconsolata` commented out (cosmetic, low risk)
  - `hyperref` loaded redundantly (no effect)
  - `xcolor` loaded redundantly (no effect)
  - 12 extra content packages added (all safe)
  - Float/display spacing overrides after `\maketitle` (common practice, technically non-default)
- **No formatting-breaking or compliance-threatening differences found.**

The official template includes it for better typewriter font aesthetics. This is cosmetic and the official template itself says \"may be commented out.\" **Low risk** -- only affects `\\texttt{}` rendering slightly.\n\n2. **`hyperref` loaded explicitly in our preamble** (line 25: `\\usepackage{hyperref}`). The official template does NOT include this because `acl.sty` already loads `hyperref` internally (via `\\RequirePackage{hyperref}` at line 302 of `acl.sty`). Loading it twice should be harmless (LaTeX silently ignores duplicate `\\usepackage` calls), but it is redundant. **No risk** -- but unnecessary.\n\n---\n\n## (d) Extra packages we added (not in official sample)\n\nThese are content packages we added for our paper. None of them modify the ACL formatting:\n\n| Package | Purpose | Risk |\n|---------|---------|------|\n| `amsmath` | Math environments | Safe |\n| `amssymb` | Math symbols | Safe |\n| `amsthm` | Theorem environments | Safe |\n| `booktabs` | Better table rules | Safe |\n| `multirow` | Multi-row table cells | Safe |\n| `xcolor` | Colors (already loaded by `acl.sty`) | Redundant but safe |\n| `enumitem` | Customized lists | Safe, but **could theoretically change list spacing** from ACL defaults if used with options that override the spacing set in `acl.sty`. Verify no global `\\setlist` overrides are used. |\n| `pifont` | Ding symbols | Safe |\n| `subcaption` | Sub-figures | Safe (compatible with `caption` loaded by `acl.sty`) |\n| `tikz` + libraries | Diagrams | Safe |\n| `listings` | Code listings | Safe |\n| `algorithm` + `algpseudocode` | Pseudocode | Safe |\n\n**One additional concern**: Lines 51-59 of `main.tex` override various spacing parameters (`\\textfloatsep`, `\\floatsep`, `\\abovecaptionskip`, `\\belowcaptionskip`, `\\abovedisplayskip`, `\\belowdisplayskip`, `\\intextsep`) to save space. These are **not** in the official template. They tighten float/display spacing beyond ACL defaults. This is a common practice and reviewers rarely flag it, but it does deviate from the official layout defaults defined in `acl.sty`.\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n- **`acl.sty`**: Byte-identical to official. <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01HRr2rtWxqqxaU7J46YvoMB`</summary>

```text
15	\looseness=-1
16	For the broader neuro-symbolic community, our results expose a structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines: when the same model generates both reasoning and formal constraints, the symbolic layer inherits the model's distributional prior rather than providing an independent check. This limitation is not specific to logical constraint extraction; it applies to any self-verification paradigm where the verifier shares the generator's knowledge boundary \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}.
17	
18	\looseness=-1
19	Cross-architecture verification escapes only when the verifier exceeds generator accuracy (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); this points to \emph{architectural diversity} over scale as the key design principle. Trained verifiers (PRM \citep{lightman2024prm,wang2024mathshepherd}, ORM \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}) succeed precisely because they break the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ chain with independently supervised signal; our NLI escape instantiates this principle without task-specific training ($+6.67$\,pp). For test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}, our findings imply that allocating additional inference budget to self-consistent sampling yields diminishing returns once $K_\text{eff}$ saturates; redirecting compute toward structurally independent verification offers a more efficient path. Lightweight trained verification is a natural next step (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
20	
```

  "text": "## 模板自检 + 页数控制 完成\n\n### (a) 差异清单\n\n**`acl.sty`**: 与官方 `github.com/acl-org/acl-style-files` 逐字节一致，MD5 完全匹配，零差异。\n\n**`acl_natbib.bst`**: 与官方逐字节一致，零差异。\n\n**`main.tex` preamble vs 官方 `acl_latex.tex`**:\n\n| 差异项 | 说明 | 风险 |\n|--------|------|------|\n| `inconsolata` 被注释掉 | 官方模板自己也说 \"may be commented out\"，仅影响 `\\texttt{}` 字体 | 无风险 |\n| `\\usepackage{hyperref}` 冗余加载 | `acl.sty` 内部已 `\\RequirePackage{hyperref}`，重复加载无效果 | 无风险 |\n| `xcolor` 冗余加载 | `acl.sty` 内部已加载 | 无风险 |\n| 12 个内容包 (amsmath, booktabs, tikz 等) | 官方样本没有但这是论文内容需要的，不影响格式 | 安全 |\n| Lines 51-59 float/display spacing 覆盖 | 缩紧浮动体间距以控制 8 页预算，常见做法 | 低风险，保留 |\n\n### (b) 修复动作\n\n- **已修复**: 删除冗余 `\\usepackage{hyperref}`（acl.sty 已加载）\n- **已修复**: 删除未使用的 `pifont` 包（移除 Figure 4 后不再需要 `\\ding`）\n- **已修复**: discussion.tex 最后一处 `$M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$` → `$M{\\to}T{\\to}(A,C)$`\n- **保留 `inconsolata` 注释**: 当前 TeX 环境不可用，且官方明确允许注释\n- **保留 spacing 覆盖**: 标准做法，不影响合规性\n- **保留 12 个内容包**: 论文内容所需\n\n### (c) 正文页数\n\n**正文（Title→§6 Discussion and Conclusion）= 恰好 8 页**。Limitations 和 References 从 p8 左栏下部开始，不计入正文页数限制。页数刚好合规。\n\n### 额外修复（Figure 4 已删除）\n\n诊断流程图已移除，§5.3 文中对 `Figure~\\ref{fig:diagnostic-flowchart}` 的引用已删除。LaTeX 自动重编号：原 Figure 5（kappa-accuracy scatter）现为 Figure 4。编译通过，零 critical warning。"

```text
1	\section{Prompt Templates}
2	\label{app:prompts}
3	
4	SICA uses domain-specific prompt templates for constraint extraction: one for mathematical reasoning (GSM8K, AIME) and one for logical reasoning (ProofWriter, FOLIO). Both share the same system prompt. The LLM is called with temperature $T{=}0.3$, max tokens $1024$, and structured JSON output mode. Up to 6 traces are processed in parallel via thread pooling, with 2 retries on failure (exponential backoff, base 0.5s).
5	
6	\paragraph{System Prompt.}\leavevmode
7	
8	\begin{lstlisting}
9	You are a constraint extraction tool. Output ONLY valid JSON. Do NOT solve problems, reason about correctness, or think step-by-step. Extract constraints from the given trace exactly as stated.
10	\end{lstlisting}
11	
12	\paragraph{Mathematical Constraint Extraction (EXTRACTION\_PROMPT).}
13	
14	Used for GSM8K and AIME datasets. The placeholder \texttt{\{trace\}} is replaced with the reasoning trace at runtime.
15	
16	\begin{lstlisting}
17	Extract mathematical constraints from this reasoning trace as a JSON object.
18	
19	Rules:
20	- type: "equation", "inequality", or "predicate"
21	- expression: human-readable, e.g. "x + y == 14"
22	- z3_formula: a single comparison expression in Python Z3 syntax
23	- source_step: step number where the constraint appears
24	- Use variable names from the trace
25	
26	z3_formula MUST follow these rules:
27	- Allowed operators: ==, !=, <, >, <=, >=, +, -, *, /, **, %
28	- Use individual named variables: a1, a2, a3... NOT arrays a[1] or sets A
29	- Expand all sums explicitly: "a1 + a2 + a3 == 55" NOT "sum([a1,a2,a3])"
30	- NO Python builtins: sum(), len(), max(), min(), list(), set(), range(), int()
31	- Each z3_formula must be exactly ONE comparison (==, !=, <, >, <=, >=)
32	- For multiple values, write separate constraints for each
33	
34	Output ONLY the JSON below, nothing else:
35	{"constraints": [{"type": "equation", "expression": "x + y == 14", "z3_formula": "x + y == 14", "source_step": 1}], "answer": "final_answer", "variables": ["x", "y"]}
36	
37	Trace:
38	{trace}
39	\end{lstlisting}
40	
41	\paragraph{Logical Constraint Extraction (LOGIC\_EXTRACTION\_PROMPT).}
42	
43	Used for ProofWriter and FOLIO datasets.
44	
45	\begin{lstlisting}
46	IMPORTANT: You are an EXTRACTION tool, NOT a problem solver.
47	- Do NOT re-solve or re-reason about the problem.
48	- Do NOT verify whether the trace's reasoning is correct.
49	- ONLY extract the logical constraints that are explicitly stated or used in the trace below.
50	- Output ONLY the JSON object, nothing else.
```

````text
1	---
2	description: 用 PaperBanana 生成高品质学术概念图（架构/流程/framework）。统计图不要用 PaperBanana，用 draw-paper-figure。需要 GOOGLE_API_KEY。
3	name: paper-banana
4	scope: global

10	  - conceptual-diagram

13	  - request_secret
14	  - poll_secret_request
15	---
16	
17	# Paper Banana — 高品质学术概念图生成
18	
19	PaperBanana 由 Gemini Pro 自动写 matplotlib 代码，VLM Critic 评估渲染结果并迭代优化，输出顶会级别 300 DPI 概念图。
20	
21	**质量底线：每张图必须达到顶会论文的美观度——高级感、专业感、精致感。绝不接受 matplotlib 默认样式的廉价感。**
22	
23	## 适用范围
24	
25	| 场景 | 用什么 |
26	|------|--------|
27	| **概念图**（架构图、流程图、framework 示意） | **PaperBanana** ← 本 skill |
28	| **统计图**（scatter、bar、line、heatmap、forest） | **draw-paper-figure.skill.md**。**不要用 PaperBanana 画统计图** |
29	| 离线 / 无 GOOGLE_API_KEY | **draw-paper-figure.skill.md** |
30	
31	## 执行环境
32	
33	**PaperBanana 在本机（EC2）运行，不要 SSH 到远程服务器。**
34	
35	EC2（ap-northeast-1 东京）可直接访问 Gemini API，无需代理。
36	
37	## 前置条件
38	
39	### Step 1：获取 GOOGLE_API_KEY
40	
41	```python
42	request_secret(key_id="google_api_key", project="&lt;project_name>", purpose="PaperBanana 概念图生成")
43	# 等待 Director 审批后
44	poll_secret_request(request_id="<返回的 request_id>", project="&lt;project_name>")
45	```
46	
47	如果密钥尚未存储，告知 Director 需要 GOOGLE_API_KEY。
48	
49	### Step 2：安装验证（每次使用前必须检查）
50	
51	```bash
52	# 检查是否已安装
53	paperbanana --version 2>/dev/null || {
54	  echo "PaperBanana 未安装，正在安装..."
55	  pip install paperbanana -q
56	  paperbanana --version
57	}
58	```
59	
60	如果 `paperbanana --version` 失败（命令不存在），必须先 `pip install paperbanana -q` 安装。版本需 ≥ 0.1.2。
61	
62	**设计前必读** `skills/global/paper-visualization.skill.md` 的概念图 L1（防丑 7 条）+ L2（叙事 6 条）+ L3 Pattern + Checklist。统计图部分不适用于 PaperBanana。
63	
64	## 命令：generate
65	
66	概念图用 `generate` 子命令。**必须显式指定 `--vlm-model gemini-2.5-pro`**（默认模型 `gemini-2.0-flash` 已废弃，不指定会崩溃）。**`--iterations 5`**。
67	
68	## 完整流程
69	
70	### Step 1：写意图描述文件
71	
72	将详细设计描述写入文件（太长不适合命令行参数）：
73	
74	```bash
75	cat > /tmp/fig_intent.txt << 'INTENT'
76	Three-panel academic figure for a top-venue paper (full width, aspect ratio ~3.2:1, figsize 14x4.5 inches):
77	
78	Panel (a) 'The Phenomenon' (left, ~38% width):
79	Grouped bar chart. TF bars: dark navy (#2C3E50). AR bars: academic red (#C0392B).
80	Exact data: Base TF=0.501 AR=0.019, ThreadGate TF=0.504 AR=0.064, ...
81	Y-axis 'Mid-turn GAR' range 0–0.55. Only left+bottom spines. Serif font 10pt.
82	Add bracket annotation '8-17x gap' between bar groups.
83	
84	Panel (b) 'Diagnostic Chain' (center, ~24% width):
85	Vertical flowchart, 4 rounded boxes connected by thin gray arrows:
86	  Step 1: light gray (#F5F6F7) box, thin border (#DEE2E6), 'Gate Analysis' + subtitle. Green ✓
87	  Step 2: same style, 'PCGrad Exclusion'. Red ✗
88	  Step 3: same style, 'Representation Check'. Red ✗
89	  Step 4: light green (#D4EDDA) background, 'Conclusion: Exposure bias'. Green ✓
90	No axes, no spines. Clean infographic style.
91	
92	Panel (c) 'Constructive Evidence' (right, ~38% width):
93	Line plot. Data points: (0, 0.064), (3, 0.556), (5, 0.727).
94	Navy line (#2C3E50), open circles (white fill, navy edge, width 2).
95	Light blue fill (#EBF5FB, alpha 0.4) under curve.
96	Annotate '11x recovery' in muted gray, 9pt italic.
97	
98	OVERALL STYLE:
99	- 300 DPI, white background, publication quality
100	- Serif font (Times New Roman or similar) throughout
101	- Panel labels '(a)', '(b)', '(c)' in 11pt bold at top-left
102	- Generous white space (wspace=0.35)
103	- Muted academic palette: navy #2C3E50, red #C0392B, green #27AE60, gray #7F8C8D
104	- NO matplotlib default styling: no gray background, no thick gridlines, no tab10 colors
105	- Drop shadows on boxes: subtle, 2px offset, 10% opacity
106	- Rounded corners 8px on all rectangles
107	- Arrow heads: small, elegant, not bulky
108	INTENT
109	```
110	
111	### Step 2：运行 generate
112	
113	```bash
114	GOOGLE_API_KEY="[REDACTED]" paperbanana generate \
115	  --input /tmp/fig_intent.txt \
116	  --caption "Three-panel diagnostic framework: (a) TF vs AR performance gap, (b) systematic diagnosis chain, (c) beta-steering recovery evidence" \
117	  --vlm-model gemini-2.5-pro \
118	  --output figures/paper/fig1_overview.png \
119	  --iterations 5
120	```
121	
122	### Step 3：视觉检查（不可跳过）
123	
124	**Read 生成的 PNG 文件**，视觉评估以下维度：
125	
126	1. **高级感**：是否有顶会论文的精致感？还是看起来像课程作业？
127	2. **颜色**：是否有语义（不是随机分配）？是否和谐、不刺眼？
128	3. **文字**：缩到 column width 后是否 ≥ 7pt 可读？
129	4. **布局**：留白是否合理？元素是否对齐？
130	5. **细节**：圆角、阴影、箭头是否精致？边框是否统一？
131	
132	**不满意 → 必须修改意图描述文件，重新生成。**
133	
134	### Step 4：转 PDF
135	
136	```bash
137	python3 -c "
138	import matplotlib; matplotlib.use('Agg')
139	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt, matplotlib.image as mpimg
140	img = mpimg.imread('figures/paper/fig1_overview.png')
141	h, w = img.shape[:2]
142	fig = plt.figure(figsize=(w/300, h/300), dpi=300)
143	ax = fig.add_axes([0,0,1,1]); ax.imshow(img); ax.axis('off')
144	plt.savefig('figures/paper/fig1_overview.pdf', bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)
145	plt.close()
146	"
147	```
148	
149	## 意图描述质量红线
150	
151	Intent 质量直接决定图的质量。必须包含以下 7 项（缺一不可）：
152	
153	1. **空间布局**：每个 panel 的宽度占比（如 38%/24%/38%）
154	2. **组件形状 + 尺寸**：圆角半径 8px、figsize (14, 4.5)
155	3. **颜色 hex 码**：不说"蓝色"，说 "#2C3E50"，绑定语义
156	4. **所有文字内容**：box label、副标题、箭头 annotation
157	5. **尺寸规格**：DPI 300、字号 10-12pt body / 8-9pt labels
158	6. **细节规格**：阴影偏移 2px / 10% opacity、箭头粗细、边框宽度
159	7. **反面约束**："NO matplotlib default styling, NO gray background, NO tab10 colors"
160	
161	### 美学风格关键词（必须在 intent 中出现）
162	
163	```
164	STYLE REQUIREMENTS:
165	- Publication quality for a TOP venue (NeurIPS/ICML/EMNLP)
166	- Elegant, premium, sophisticated — NOT cheap or homework-like
167	- Subtle drop shadows (2px, 10% opacity) on boxes
168	- Rounded corners (8px) on all rectangles
169	- Thin, elegant arrow heads — not bulky matplotlib defaults
170	- Serif font (Times) for labels, sans-serif (Helvetica) for annotations
171	- Muted, harmonious color palette — NO saturated primary colors
172	- Generous white space between elements
173	- Consistent visual weight across all panels
174	```
175	
176	### 反面教材（这样的 intent 一定画不好）
177	
178	- "Draw a pipeline diagram with three stages"
179	- "Architecture diagram for our system"
180	
181	太模糊。LLM 会用 matplotlib 默认样式填充所有你没指定的细节，结果就是廉价感。
182	
183	## 迭代策略（至少 3 轮外部迭代，Hero Figure 至少 5 轮）
184	
185	PaperBanana 内部有 VLM Critic 迭代（`--iterations 5`），但这还不够。你必须做**外部迭代**：
186	
187	### 第 1 轮：基础生成
188	运行 PaperBanana → Read PNG → 识别大方向问题（布局、配色、结构）
189	
190	### 第 2 轮：结构修复
191	修改意图描述（布局占比、颜色 hex、组件关系）→ 重新运行 → Read PNG
192	
193	### 第 3 轮：细节打磨
194	修改意图描述（字号、阴影、对齐、箭头粗细）→ 重新运行 → Read PNG
195	
196	### 第 4 轮（Hero Figure）：风格统一
197	线宽 / 字号 / 间距全图统一？与论文其他图配色一致？→ 重新运行 → Read PNG
198	
199	### 第 5 轮（Hero Figure）：最终 polish
200	像素级检查 → 缩到 column width 后文字 ≥ 7pt？→ 终极判断：放在 NeurIPS best paper 里违和吗？
201	
202	### 判断标准
203	
204	**可以交付**：看起来像 NeurIPS 2025 best paper 里的图。
205	**不可交付**：看起来像 matplotlib gallery 的示例图。
206	
207	## 失败回退
208	
209	如果 PaperBanana 多次崩溃（API 错误、模型不兼容）：
210	
211	1. 确认 `--vlm-model gemini-2.5-pro` 是否指定
212	2. 确认 GOOGLE_API_KEY 是否有效：`curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' "https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models?key=$GOOGLE_API_KEY"` 应返回 200
213	3. 如果仍然失败，回退 matplotlib 手写：
214	   - Read `skills/global/paper-visualization.skill.md` 获取 L1-L4 设计标准
215	   - 写 Python 脚本手动画概念图（FancyBboxPatch、FancyArrowPatch 等）
216	   - **同样必须 Read PNG 做视觉检查，至少迭代 3 轮**
217	   - **同样必须达到顶会级别的美观度**
218	   - 保存 .py 脚本到 figures/paper/ 目录便于后续修改
219	
````

5`），但这还不够。你必须做**外部迭代**：\n\n### 第 1 轮：基础生成\n运行 PaperBanana → Read PNG → 识别大方向问题（布局、配色、结构）\n\n### 第 2 轮：结构修复\n修改意图描述（布局占比、颜色 hex、组件关系）→ 重新运行 → Read PNG\n\n### 第 3 轮：细节打磨\n修改意图描述（字号、阴影、对齐、箭头粗细）→ 重新运行 → Read PNG\n\n### 第 4 轮（Hero Figure）：风格统一\n线宽 / 字号 / 间距全图统一？与论文其他图配色一致？→ 重新运行 → Read PNG\n\n### 第 5 轮（Hero Figure）：最终 polish\n像素级检查 → 缩到 column width 后文字 ≥ 7pt？→ 终极判断：放在 NeurIPS best paper 里违和吗？\n\n### 判断标准\n\n**可以交付**：看起来像 NeurIPS 2025 best paper 里的图。\n**不可交付**：看起来像 matplotlib gallery 的示例图。\n\n## 失败回退\n\n如果 PaperBanana 多次崩溃（API 错误、模型不兼容）：\n\n1. 确认 `--vlm-model gemini-2.5-pro` 是否指定\n2. 确认 GOOGLE_API_KEY 是否有效：`curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \"https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models?key=$GOOGLE_API_KEY\"` 应返回 200\n3. 如果仍然失败，回退 matplotlib 手写：\n   - Read `skills/global/paper-visualization.skill.md` 获取 L1-L4 设计标准\n   - 写 Python 脚本手动画概念图（FancyBboxPatch、FancyArrowPatch 等）\n   - **同样必须 Read PNG 做视觉检查，至少迭代 3 轮**\n   - **同样必须达到顶会级别的美观度**\n   - 保存 .py 脚本到 figures/paper/ 目录便于后续修改\n",

    }

```text
565	\section{Failure Mode Analysis}
566	\label{app:failure-modes}
567	\label{sec:failure-modes}
568	
569	The regime-dependent pattern in Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reflects three distinct failure modes of self-extracted logical constraints.
570	
571	\paragraph{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\geq$ $\sim$60\%).} Strong models produce consistent reasoning traces, and constraints extracted from those traces mirror the answer distribution. When most constraints support the same candidate, MAX-SAT excludes few clauses ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), the penalty term in Eq.~\ref{eq:score} vanishes, and the scoring function degenerates to weighted vote counting (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). SICA and SC then produce identical predictions. The ablation in Appendix~\ref{app:ablation} confirms this empirically: on Qwen2.5-14B (FOLIO + ProofWriter, $n = 804$), MAX-SAT and count-only baselines agree on 803 of 804 predictions.
572	
573	\paragraph{Type B: non-formalizable domains.} LogiQA problems require pragmatic and contextual reasoning that resists clean logical formalization. Qwen2.5-14B on LogiQA achieves an extraction rate of 86.4\%, but the resulting constraints have a contradiction rate of only 0.8\%: extracted formulas rarely conflict across traces, leaving MAX-SAT with no logical tension to exploit. Mistral-7B on LogiQA sits in the same SC range (50.00\%) yet produces $\Delta = +1.50$\,pp ($p = 0.581$); Qwen2.5-14B on the same domain yields $\Delta = -1.00$\,pp ($p = 0.625$). Neither model benefits, confirming that the right accuracy regime is necessary but not sufficient when the domain resists formalization.
574	
575	\paragraph{Type C: error amplification (SC $\leq$ $\sim$40\%).} When model accuracy is low, the majority of traces reach incorrect answers. Constraints extracted from wrong traces encode incorrect reasoning as formal rules. Z3 treats these rules as soft clauses with weight proportional to their frequency, so the solver preferentially satisfies the wrong constraints and penalizes traces that happen to reach the correct answer. Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($\Delta = -2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.042$) demonstrates this effect: SICA converts a weak positive signal in trace diversity into a net negative by formalizing the errors.
576	
577	
578	\section{Cross-Model Confirmation Bias}
579	\label{app:cross-model-bias}
580	
581	Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias} reports MaxSAT-weighted confirmation bias metrics across three model families on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Weighted bias ratio and wrong-supporting constraint percentage both decrease with model capability, while SICA's accuracy effect shifts from positive (Mistral, intermediate-SC regime) to negative (LLaMA, high-SC regime). Raw bias ratios ($4.97\times$, $3.85\times$, $2.88\times$) are reported in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}. LLaMA weighted BR CI: [2.19, 3.28].
582	
583	\begin{table}[h]
584	\centering
585	\small
586	\caption{Cross-model confirmation bias on FOLIO ($n = 204$, $K = 12$). BR (wtd.): MaxSAT-weighted ratio of error-supporting constraints on incorrect vs.\ correct problems. Wrong \%: weighted fraction of constraints supporting the incorrect majority on error problems. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp).}
587	\label{tab:cross-model-bias}
588	\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}
589	\toprule
590	Model & BR (wtd.) & Wrong (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
591	\midrule
592	Mistral-7B & $4.24\times$ & 80.9 & $+$4.90 \\
593	Qwen2.5-14B & $3.69\times$ & 78.7 & $-$0.98 \\
594	LLaMA-3.1-8B & $2.66\times$ & 72.7 & $-$4.41 \\
595	\bottomrule
596	\end{tabular}
597	\end{table}
598	
599	\paragraph{Frontier model universality.}
600	\label{app:frontier-scb}
601	Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb} extends confirmation bias analysis to nine models spanning 7B open-source to frontier closed-source. All models exhibit $\text{BR} > 1$ (range: $1.66$--$4.97\times$). Extraction context varies: open-source models use self-extraction (the model extracts constraints from its own traces); frontier models use cross-model extraction (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from the target model's traces), as closed-source APIs do not expose internal representations suitable for direct symbolic extraction.
602	
603	\begin{table}[h]
604	\centering
605	\small
606	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
607	\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $n_\text{err}$: number of incorrect instances on which BR is computed. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). $^\dagger$8 API-failure questions excluded ($n = 196$). All models exhibit $\text{BR} > 1$; BR estimates for high-accuracy models ($n_\text{err} < 40$) have wider confidence intervals; Gemini ($n_\text{err} \approx 12$) should be interpreted with particular caution. Process-level similarity ($\kappa_{\text{proc}}$) ranges from $0.25$ (R1-Distill) to $0.79$ (gpt-4o/4.1/Gemini); see Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}.}

627	\paragraph{ProcessSim definition.}
628	\label{app:process-sim}
629	Process similarity is the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
630	\begin{equation}
631	\label{eq:process-sim}
632	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
633	\end{equation}
634	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$.
635	
636	\paragraph{Process-$\kappa$ gradient.}
637	\label{app:process-gradient}
638	Under cross-model extraction (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from other models' traces), SICA gain decreases monotonically with process-level cosine similarity (Table~\ref{tab:process-gradient}; Spearman $\rho = -1.0$, $n = 5$, one-tailed $p = 0.0083$). Gain requires two conditions: diverse traces (low $\kappa_{\text{proc}}$) \emph{and} substantial self-bias (high BR). R1-Distill-8B satisfies both ($\kappa_{\text{proc}} = 0.248$, $\text{BR} = 2.92$) and gains $+2.45$\,pp; o3 has moderate trace diversity ($\kappa_{\text{proc}} = 0.596$) but near-minimal bias ($\text{BR} = 1.66$, exhibiting a bimodal error pattern: 82\% of error problems have $\text{BR} = \infty$ but low finite-BR), yielding $\Delta = 0$.

Spearman $\rho(\kappa_{\text{proc}} \rightarrow \Delta) = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$. $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$).}

Process-level similarity ($\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$) ranges from $0.25$ (R1-Distill) to $0.79$ (gpt-4o/4.1/Gemini); see Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}.}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $n_\\text{err}$ & BR & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & 90 & 4.97 & 0.00 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \\textrm{---} & \\textrm{---} & 2.88 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.98 & 49 & 3.85 & $-$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & 52 & 2.92 & $+$2.45 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 76.96 & 47 & 2.65 & $-$1.96 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & 35 & 2.70 & $-$1.47 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & 32 & 3.48 & $+$0.49 \\\\\no3$^\\dagger$ & $^b$ & 82.65 & 34 & 1.66 & 0.00 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & 12 & 2.79 & $-$1.05 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{ProcessSim definition.}\n\\label{app:process-sim}\nProcess similarity is the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$.\n\n\\paragraph{Process-$\\kappa$ gradient.}\n\\label{app:process-gradient}\nUnder cross-model extraction (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from other models' traces), SICA gain decreases monotonically with process-level cosine similarity (Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}; Spearman $\\rho = -1.0$, $n = 5$, one-tailed $p = 0.0083$). Gain requires two conditions: diverse traces (low $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$) \\emph{and} substantial self-bias (high BR). R1-Distill-8B satisfies both ($\\kappa_{\\text{proc}} = 0.248$, $\\text{BR} = 2.92$) and gains $+2.45$\\,pp; o3 has moderate trace diversity ($\\kappa_{\\text{proc}} = 0.596$) but near-minimal bias ($\\text{BR} = 1.66$, exhibiting a bimodal error pattern: 82\\% of error problems have $\\text{BR} = \\infty$ but low finite-BR), yielding $\\Delta = 0$.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Process-$\\kappa$ gradient under cross-model extraction (Mistral-7B $\\rightarrow$ target model traces, FOLIO). $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$: process-level cosine similarity. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). Spearman $\\rho(\\kappa_{\\text{proc}} \\rightarrow \\Delta) = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$. $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$).}\n\\label{tab:process-gradient}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$ & $\\Delta$ (pp) & BR \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-Distill-8B & 0.248 & $+$2.45 & 2.92 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & 0.563 & $+$0.49 & 3.48 \\\\\no3 & 0.596 & 0.00 & 1.66 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & 0.790 & $-$1.05 & 2.79 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & 0.792 & $-$1.47 & 2.70 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Independence-Accuracy Scatter Plot}\n\\label{app:kappa-scatter}\n\nThe independence-accuracy scatter plot is presented in Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy} in the main text.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]",

```text
276	\section{Case Studies: Discordant Predictions}
277	\label{app:case-studies}
278	
279	Among 804 problems, SICA and SC agree on 773 (96.1\%). The 31 discordant cases comprise 16 SICA wins (SICA correct, SC wrong), 14 SC wins (SC correct, SICA wrong), and 1 where both select different wrong answers. All 31 occur at exact ties (entropy${=}1.0$, 6:6 vote split) or near-ties (entropy${\geq}0.9183$, 8:4 at most), since SICA can differ from SC only when constraint weights shift the majority away from SC's tiebreak choice. We present three representative cases that illustrate the mechanism behind capability-accuracy constraint failures.
280	
281	\paragraph{Case 1: SICA Wins (folio\_134, FOLIO).}
282	
283	\emph{Premises}: Some mammals have teeth. Platypus have no teeth. Platypus are mammals. Humans have teeth. \emph{Conclusion}: Humans are mammals. \emph{Gold}: Unknown.
284	
285	The premises state that \emph{some} mammals have teeth and that humans have teeth, but ``having teeth'' is not sufficient for ``being a mammal'' under open-world semantics. SC produces a 6:6 split (Unknown:6, True:6) and tiebreaks alphabetically to True (wrong). SICA extracts 7 constraints; MaxSAT excludes 1 constraint inconsistent with the rest, reducing the weighted score for True-voting traces and tipping the aggregation to Unknown (correct). The excluded constraint likely captured an incorrect inference step (e.g., affirming the consequent) present only in True-voting traces. While SICA succeeds here, the success is coincidental: the excluded constraint happens to anti-correlate with the correct answer.
286	
287	\paragraph{Case 2: SC Wins (ProofWriter-1066\_Q6, ProofWriter).}
288	
289	\emph{Key chain}: White(Bob)$\to$Round(Bob) [white things are round] $\to$ Young(Bob) [all round things are young]. \emph{Conclusion}: ``Bob is not young.'' \emph{Gold}: False (Bob IS young).
290	
291	SC produces a 6:6 split (False:6, True:6) and tiebreaks to False (correct). SICA extracts 17 constraints; MaxSAT excludes 2 that appear preferentially in False-voting traces. These excluded constraints likely captured correct intermediate derivations (e.g., \texttt{young\_bob == True}) that appeared only in traces completing the full deduction chain. By excluding them, MaxSAT removes evidence supporting the correct answer. SICA selects True (wrong). This case illustrates how self-extracted constraints can systematically mislead: correct reasoning steps become discriminative signals that the solver mistakenly penalizes.
292	
293	\paragraph{Case 3: Non-discriminative Constraints (ProofWriter-903\_Q6, ProofWriter).}
294	
295	\emph{Key chain}: Nice(Mouse)$\to$Cold(Mouse) [all nice people are cold] $\to$ Chases(Mouse,Mouse) [nice and cold $\to$ chase mouse]. \emph{Conclusion}: ``The mouse does not chase the mouse.'' \emph{Gold}: False.
296	
297	SC produces a 6:6 split (False:6, Unknown:6) and tiebreaks to False (correct). SICA extracts 44 constraints but excludes only 1 (2.3\%). The remaining 43 are domain facts and grounded rules (\texttt{nice\_mouse == True}, \texttt{Implies(nice\_mouse, cold\_mouse)}, etc.) that Z3 satisfies identically regardless of the final answer. Both False-voting and Unknown-voting traces share the same premises and grounded rules; they differ only in whether the trace completed the full derivation chain. The single excluded constraint carries negligible weight against 43 shared constraints, so SICA collapses to SC.
298	
299	\paragraph{Pattern.}
300	
301	These cases exemplify the general pattern across the 773 agreeing problems: the vast majority of extracted constraints encode shared domain structure rather than answer-specific reasoning, and MaxSAT aggregation degenerates to majority vote. Table~\ref{tab:case_summary} summarizes.
302	
303	\begin{table}[h]
304	\centering
305	\small
306	\caption{Summary of three representative discordant cases. All occur at exact 6:6 vote ties. Constraint exclusion can break ties in either direction (Cases 1--2) or have no effect (Case 3).}
307	\label{tab:case_summary}
308	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
309	\begin{tabular}{@{}lllcccc@{}}
310	\toprule
311	Case & ID & Gold & SC & SICA & \#Constr. & Excl.\ (\%) \\
312	\midrule
313	1 (SICA win) & folio\_134 & Unknown & True $\times$ & Unknown $\checkmark$ & 7 & 1 (14.3\%) \\
314	2 (SC win) & PW-1066\_Q6 & False & False $\checkmark$ & True $\times$ & 17 & 2 (11.8\%) \\
315	3 (Non-disc.) & PW-903\_Q6 & False & False $\checkmark$ & False $\checkmark$ & 44 & 1 (2.3\%) \\
316	\bottomrule
317	\end{tabular}}
318	\end{table}
319	
320	
321	\section{Oracle Control}
322	\label{app:oracle}
323	\label{sec:oracle}
324	\label{app:pw-oracle-subtype}
325	
326	Replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+13.73$\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\Delta$ ($+3.43$\,pp) captures only 11.5\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.
327	
328	\paragraph{ProofWriter per-subtype analysis.} ProofWriter problems encode deductive rules as Domain-Specific Language (DSL) logic programs. Each problem uses one of four predicate subtypes: attribute predicates with or without negation (AttNeg, AttNoneg), and relation predicates with or without negation (RelNeg, RelNoneg). Relation predicates involve binary relations between entities (e.g., \texttt{eats(bear, squirrel)}), while attribute predicates assign unary properties (e.g., \texttt{kind(anne)}).
329	
330	The oracle pipeline parses these DSL annotations deterministically into Z3 formulas without any LLM involvement. Of the 600 ProofWriter problems, 594 (99.0\%) produce valid Z3 parses; the remaining 6 fail due to unsupported DSL constructs. Across all 7{,}128 trace-problem pairs submitted to Z3, the solver returns a result in every case (0\% solver errors). Z3 evaluations agree with ground-truth labels on 577 of 594 parseable problems (97.14\%), confirming that the deterministic DSL-to-Z3 translation faithfully preserves problem semantics.
331	
332	Table~\ref{tab:pw_oracle_subtype} reports the per-subtype breakdown. Relation-predicate subtypes show substantially larger gains than attribute-predicate subtypes: RelNeg improves by $+28.58$~pp and RelNoneg by $+23.29$~pp, compared to $+10.00$~pp for AttNeg and $+8.44$~pp for AttNoneg. Relation predicates involve longer reasoning chains with more intermediate entities, producing greater answer diversity across traces ($C_0$) and more opportunities for oracle constraints to correct errors.
333	
334	\begin{table}[h]
335	\centering
336	\caption{ProofWriter oracle experiment per-subtype breakdown. Oracle-SICA uses gold DSL annotations parsed deterministically to Z3 for consistency checking. Relation-predicate subtypes (RelNeg, RelNoneg) show larger gains than attribute-predicate subtypes; relation subtypes involve longer reasoning chains and benefit more from oracle constraints. Bold indicates best accuracy per row.}
337	\label{tab:pw_oracle_subtype}
338	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
339	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
340	\toprule
341	Subtype & $n$ & SC Acc (\%) & Oracle Acc (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
342	\midrule
343	AttNoneg & 154 & 90.26 & \textbf{98.70} & +8.44 \\
344	AttNeg & 160 & 81.88 & \textbf{91.88} & +10.00 \\
345	RelNoneg & 146 & 60.96 & \textbf{84.25} & +23.29 \\
346	RelNeg & 140 & 60.71 & \textbf{89.29} & +28.58 \\
347	\midrule
348	Overall & 600 & 74.00 & \textbf{91.17} & +17.17 \\
349	\bottomrule
350	\end{tabular}}
351	\end{table}
352	
353	
354	\section{Constraint Ablation Details}
355	\label{app:ablation}
```

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	\label{sec:pipeline}
4	\label{sec:condorcet}
5	\label{sec:effective-k}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}), the effective ensemble size $K_\text{eff}$ is
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = K \,/\, [1 + (K{-}1) \cdot \kappa]
11	\end{equation}
12	(following \citet{ladha1992correlated}). Intuitively, $K_\text{eff}$ measures how many effectively independent votes $K$ correlated traces contribute; e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\text{eff} \approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.
13	
14	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	
16	\begin{table*}[t]
17	\centering
18	\small
19	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). Two conditions show significant degradation after BH correction ($m{=}6$ confirmatory; remaining exploratory); no condition shows a significant gain. $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\,BH adj.\ $p = 0.042$; $^{3s}$\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate; $^\dagger$\,4-seed replication in Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed} (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\%$ for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\%$: QwQ-32B AIME$^\ddagger$ $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K$^\ddagger$ $n{=}30$) yield $\Delta = 0$ and are reported separately in Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}. $^\ddagger$Pilot conditions ($n{\leq}30$); included for coverage, not confirmatory evidence. Single-seed conditions use seed 123 (not selected post hoc).}
20	\label{tab:main_results}
21	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}
22	\toprule
23	Model & Domain & $n$ & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
24	\midrule
25	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\leq$ 40\%)}} \\
26	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\
27	\midrule

46	Qwen3-14B$^\dagger$ & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\

49	\end{tabular}

51	
52	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
53	
54	\looseness=-1
55	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers. The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
56	
57	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
58	\begin{equation}
59	\label{eq:maxsat}
60	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
61	\end{equation}
62	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout (Algorithm~\ref{alg:maxsat}; Appendix~\ref{app:z3-pseudocode}). Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score; SICA is thus a strict generalization of SC.
63	
64	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
65	\begin{equation}
66	\label{eq:score}
67	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
68	\end{equation}
69	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
70	
71	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
72	\begin{equation}
73	\label{eq:degenerate}
74	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
75	\end{equation}
76	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
77	
78	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a lightweight diagnostic computable from pilot traces: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\pm}0.49$\,pp across $\alpha \in [0, 1]$).
79	
80	\paragraph{Bias ratio.} We quantify extraction bias via the \emph{bias ratio} (BR):
81	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mirage}
82	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
83	\end{equation}
84	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's majority answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. BR quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1): values above 1 reflect the trace-count base rate inherent to source-attributed constraints. $\text{BR} = 1$ indicates no coupling beyond chance. We validate this metric empirically in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}.
85

Single-seed conditions use seed 123 (not selected post hoc).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 50.00 & 51.50 & $+$1.50 & 0.581 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.01 & 73.78 & $-$1.24 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 79.00 & 78.00 & $-$1.00 & 0.625 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 83.33 & 84.80 & $+$1.47 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B$^\\dagger$ & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout (Algorithm~\\ref{alg:maxsat}; Appendix~\\ref{app:z3-pseudocode}). Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score; SICA is thus a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a lightweight diagnostic computable from pilot traces: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\paragraph{Bias ratio.} We quantify extraction bias via the \\emph{bias ratio} (BR):\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's majority answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. BR quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1): values above 1 reflect the trace-count base rate inherent to source-attributed constraints. $\\text{BR} = 1$ indicates no coupling beyond chance. We validate this metric empirically in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}.\n",
      "numLines": 85,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 85
    }

{
  "text": "用 PaperBanana 重画 Figure 1（Pipeline 图）和 Figure 3（Causal DAG 图），要求顶会级别精美度。\n\n**统一配色方案（两张图必须一致）**：\n- 主色 navy: #2C3E50\n- 强调红（退化/失败）: #C0392B\n- 强调绿（成功/oracle）: #27AE60\n- 次要灰: #7F8C8D\n- 浅蓝填充: #EBF5FB\n- 浅灰填充: #F5F6F7\n- 边框: #DEE2E6\n\n**前置步骤**：\n1. Read `skills/global/paper-banana.skill.md` 获取完整流程\n2. GOOGLE_API_KEY 已存储（key_id=\"google_api_key\"），通过 `request_secret` 获取\n3. 确保 paperbanana 已安装（`pip install paperbanana -q`）\n4. **每张图至少 3 轮外部迭代**，Read PNG 视觉检查后修改 intent 重新生成\n\n---\n\n## Figure 1: SICA Pipeline（全宽 figure*，figsize ~14x4 inches）\n\n**当前文件**: `figures/fig1_pipeline_v10.pdf`\n**替换为**: `figures/fig1_pipeline_v11.pdf` + `.png`\n\n**内容描述**（写入 intent 文件时请细化为完整 PaperBanana intent 格式，包含精确 hex 码、px 规格、字号等 7 项必备要素）：\n\n左侧输入区：\n- 圆角矩形 \"Question q\"，浅灰填充\n- 向下箭头到 \"LLM M\" 节点（navy 填充白字）\n- 向下展开为 K=12 个 trace 的扇形（用 t₁, t₂, ..., tₖ 示意，3-4 个小圆角矩形错开排列即可）\n\n中间分叉区：\n- 上路径（SC）：traces → \"Majority Vote\" 圆角矩形 → \"SC Answer\" 输出\n- 下路径（SICA）：traces → \"Constraint Extraction f(tᵢ)\" → \"Dedup (46%)\" → \"MAX-SAT (Z3)\" → \"Score(a)\" → \"SICA Answer\" 输出\n- **红色虚线框**包围 \"Constraint Extraction\" 节点，标注 \"Independence Bottleneck\"（这是论文核心发现）\n- 从 \"Gold FOL\" 节点有**绿色虚线箭头**指向 MAX-SAT，标注 \"Oracle variant\"\n\n右侧输出区：\n- SC Answer 和 SICA Answer 并列显示\n\n**关键视觉要求**：\n- 红色虚线框是全图视觉焦点，要醒目但不刺眼\n- SC 路径简短直接（占空间少），SICA 路径详细（占空间多）\n- 箭头细长优雅，不粗笨\n- 节点内文字清晰，缩到 textwidth 后 ≥ 7pt\n\n---\n\n## Figure 3: Causal DAG（全宽 figure*，figsize ~12x4 inches）\n\n**当前文件**: `figures/fig3_causal_dag_v11.tex`（TikZ standalone）→ `figures/fig3_causal_dag_v11.pdf`\n**替换为**: `figures/fig3_causal_dag_v12.pdf` + `.png`\n\n**内容描述**：\n\n水平布局的因果图，从左到右：\n- Y 节点（ground truth）：虚线圆，浅蓝填充 #EBF5FB，标签 \"Y\"\n- → 浅灰箭头标注 \"q\" → M 节点（model）：实线圆，白色填充，navy 边框\n- → 箭头标注 \"sample K\" → T₁:ₖ 节点（traces）：实线圆\n- T 节点分叉为两条路径：\n  - 上方箭头标注 \"aᵢ = h(tᵢ)\" → A 节点（answers）\n  - 下方箭头标注 \"cᵢ = f(tᵢ)\" → C 节点（constraints）\n- A 和 C 都指向 → Score 节点（圆角矩形，浅灰填充）\n\n**关键视觉元素**：\n- **红色虚线箭头** 从 A 到 C，标注 \"empirical degeneration (Obs. 1)\"——这表示 under degeneration 时 C 被 A 决定\n- A→C 虚线箭头用 #C0392B 红色\n- C 节点下方标注信息论约束：I(C; Y) ≤ I(A; Y)，小字标注 \"under degeneration\"\n- 节点之间留足白空间\n- 整体风格与 Figure 1 完全一致（同样的圆角、阴影、字体、线宽）\n\n---\n\n**输出要求**：\n1. 每张图生成后 Read PNG 做视觉检查\n2. 生成 PDF 版本（用 skill 里的 matplotlib 转换脚本）\n3. 更新 `introduction.tex` 中 Figure 1 的文件名引用\n4. 更新 `experiments.tex` 中 Figure 3 的引用（当前是 `\\input{figures/fig3_causal_dag_v11}`，改为 `\\includegraphics`）\n5. 编译验证无 critical warning\n\n两张图完成后汇报。",

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\geq}99\%$ of problems (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (three primary generators plus six additional models; BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$; Appendix~\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
8	
9	\smallskip\noindent
10	\textbf{Observation 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\;%
11	Let $t_1, \ldots, t_K \overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim} P_M(\cdot \mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\{i : a_i = a\}|$.
12	Under (i)~the degeneration condition $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\arg\max_a \operatorname{score}(a) = \arg\max_a n_a$.
13	
14	\smallskip\noindent
15	\textit{Empirical support.}\;%
16	Z3 rejection rate $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}|/|\mathcal{C}| \approx 0.034$ confirms $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral (constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups), and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
17	
18	\label{sec:process-kappa}
19	\looseness=-1 The $M{\to}T{\to}(A,C)$ generation structure provides empirical motivation \citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration (Observation~1), constraints carry no more task-relevant information than answers alone, i.e., $I(C;\, Y) \leq I(A;\, Y)$ (Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\ref{app:cil}).
20	
21	\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}
22	\label{sec:remediation}
23	\label{sec:interventions}
24	
25	\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\kappa = 0.15$, $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\kappa$ (${\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\ref{app:intervention-individual}).
26	
27	\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}
28	\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}
29	\label{sec:diagnostics}
30	
31	\looseness=-1 Three lightweight diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\geq 60\%$, BR $> 1$ $\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\leq 40\%$ $\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.
32	
33	\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\Delta = -4.41$\,pp) and non-significance ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).
34	
35	\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}
36	\label{sec:cross-arch}
37	
38	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli} for natural language inference (NLI). Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.
39	
40	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}); corrective signal must therefore originate outside the model's distribution.
41	
42	\begin{table}[t]
43	\centering
44	\small
45	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}
46	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
47	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
48	\toprule
49	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
50	\midrule
51	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
52	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.665 \\
53	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.503 \\
54	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.453 \\
55	\bottomrule
56	\end{tabular}}
57	\end{table}
58	
59	\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\sim}$72\% (Appendix~\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does; structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\ref{app:drc}).
60	
61

Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb} reports SCB across nine models spanning 7B open-source to frontier closed-source, all exhibiting $\\text{BR} > 1$ ($1.66$--$4.97\\times$). Open-source models use self-extraction; frontier models use cross-model extraction (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). The bias also holds across all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2})."

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (three primary generators plus six additional models; BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral (constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups), and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}(A,C)$ generation structure provides empirical motivation \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration (Observation~1), constraints carry no more task-relevant information than answers alone, i.e., $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three lightweight diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for natural language inference (NLI). Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}); corrective signal must therefore originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does; structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\\geq}99\\%$ of problems (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n{=}204$).
