Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n{=}95$). $^\\dagger$$n{=}196$ (8 API failures excluded).}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $n_\\text{err}$ & BR & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & 90 & 4.97 & 0.00 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & $^a$ & --- & --- & 2.88 & --- \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & $^a$ & 75.98 & 49 & 3.85 & $-$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & 52 & 2.92 & $+$2.45 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 76.96 & 47 & 2.65 & $-$1.96 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & 35 & 2.70 & $-$1.47 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & 32 & 3.48 & $+$0.49 \\\\\no3$^\\dagger$ & $^b$ & 82.65 & 34 & 1.66 & 0.00 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & 12 & 2.79 & $-$1.05 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 The pattern generalizes beyond locally-run models. Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb} reports SCB across nine models spanning 7B open-source to frontier closed-source, all exhibiting $\\text{BR} > 1$ ($1.66$--$4.97\\times$). Open-source models use self-extraction; frontier models use cross-model extraction (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). The bias also holds across all cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}).\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textbf{Observation 1} \\textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\\;%\nLet $t_1, \\ldots, t_K \\overset{\\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\\sim} P_M(\\cdot \\mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\\{i : a_i = a\\}|$.\nUnder (i)~the degeneration condition $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\\arg\\max_a \\operatorname{score}(a) = \\arg\\max_a n_a$.\n\n\\smallskip\\noindent\n\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nZ3 rejection rate $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$ confirms $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral (constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups), and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).\n\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\\looseness=-1 The $M{\\to}T{\\to}(A,C)$ generation structure provides empirical motivation \\citep{cover2006elements}: under degeneration (Observation~1), constraints carry no more task-relevant information than answers alone, i.e., $I(C;\\, Y) \\leq I(A;\\, Y)$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Process-level overlap compounds the bottleneck: mean reasoning similarity reaches $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$--$0.51$; Appendix~\\ref{app:process-sim}), and SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.0083$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Why Remediation Fails}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free variants spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}); a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges. Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\\kappa$ (${\\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Three lightweight diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run: (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\\geq 60\\%$, BR $> 1$ $\\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\\leq 40\\%$ $\\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\\Delta = -4.41$\\,pp) and non-significance ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\\,pp for Mistral-7B on ProofWriter). The three diagnostics thus enable practitioners to rule out self-extraction pipelines before full-scale deployment (Appendix~\\ref{app:diagnostic-toolkit}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Escape}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method escapes the distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). We test whether a structurally independent verifier can break the bottleneck using DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli} for natural language inference (NLI). Each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes.\n\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +1.47$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}); corrective signal must therefore originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \\emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does; structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\\ref{app:drc}).\n\n",

598	
599	\paragraph{Frontier model universality.}
600	\label{app:frontier-scb}
601	Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb} extends confirmation bias analysis to nine models spanning 7B open-source to frontier closed-source. All models exhibit $\text{BR} > 1$ (range: $1.66$--$4.97\times$). Extraction context varies: open-source models use self-extraction (the model extracts constraints from its own traces); frontier models use cross-model extraction (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from the target model's traces), as closed-source APIs do not expose internal representations suitable for direct symbolic extraction.
602	
603	\begin{table}[h]
604	\centering
605	\small
606	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
607	\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $n_\text{err}$: number of incorrect instances on which BR is computed. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). $^\dagger$8 API-failure questions excluded ($n = 196$). All models exhibit $\text{BR} > 1$; BR estimates for high-accuracy models ($n_\text{err} < 40$) have wider confidence intervals; Gemini ($n_\text{err} \approx 12$) should be interpreted with particular caution. Process-level similarity ($\kappa_{\text{proc}}$) ranges from $0.25$ (R1-Distill) to $0.79$ (gpt-4o/4.1/Gemini); see Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}.}
608	\label{tab:frontier-scb}
609	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}
610	\toprule
611	Model & Ext. & SC (\%) & $n_\text{err}$ & BR & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
612	\midrule
613	Mistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & 90 & 4.97 & 0.00 \\
614	LLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \textrm{---} & \textrm{---} & 2.88 & \textrm{---} \\
615	Qwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.98 & 49 & 3.85 & $-$0.49 \\
616	\addlinespace
617	R1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & 52 & 2.92 & $+$2.45 \\
618	gpt-4o & $^a$ & 76.96 & 47 & 2.65 & $-$1.96 \\
619	gpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & 35 & 2.70 & $-$1.47 \\
620	gpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & 32 & 3.48 & $+$0.49 \\
621	o3$^\dagger$ & $^b$ & 82.65 & 34 & 1.66 & 0.00 \\
622	Gemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & 12 & 2.79 & $-$1.05 \\
623	\bottomrule
624	\end{tabular}}
625	\end{table}
626	
627	\paragraph{ProcessSim definition.}

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/appendix.tex:1499:Since Observation~1 shows SICA reduces to majority vote under degeneration, $\mathrm{BR} \geq 1$ follows from the trace-count geometry; the empirical BR consistently exceeds this floor ($1.66\times$--$4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), reflecting both base-rate effects and extraction alignment.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/appendix.tex:1504:\emph{Bias Ratio} (BR; \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}): for each problem, compute the fraction of constraints supporting the majority answer, divided by majority vote share. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints are biased toward the majority; $\text{BR} \gg 2$ (as in all nine models tested; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) signals that extraction amplifies rather than checks the model's prior. Report as mean $\pm$ bootstrap 95\% CI.

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.7\\textwidth]{f2_regime_scatter_v2}\n\\caption{SICA $\\Delta$ (pp) vs.\\ SC baseline accuracy. Color encodes model family; shape encodes dataset (\\scalebox{0.7}{$\\blacksquare$}\\,PW, \\scalebox{0.7}{$\\blacktriangle$}\\,FOLIO, \\scalebox{0.7}{$\\bullet$}\\,LogiQA). Circled points are significant ($p < .05$). The grey band marks the $\\pm 2$\\,pp equivalence zone; connected points show seed-level variance for Mistral-7B$\\times$FOLIO. Three regimes emerge: Type~C (error amplification), intermediate, and Type~A (confirmation bias). No condition escapes the equivalence band with a positive effect.}\n\\label{fig:regime-scatter}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$, adj.\\ $p = 0.042$). No condition shows a significant positive effect. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\\times$ more often (139 vs.\\ 53 across conditions); constraint-based reweighting thus introduces noise rather than corrective signal (representative cases in Appendix~\\ref{app:case-studies}; Appendix~\\ref{app:subpopulation}).\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig3_causal_dag_v11.pdf}\n\\caption{Causal DAG of the self-extraction pipeline. Traces $T$ produce both answers $A$ and constraints $C$; under degeneration (Observation~1), $C$ is determined by $A$ (dashed arrow), yielding $I(C;Y) \\leq I(A;Y)$.}\n\\label{fig:causal-dag}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure across SC baselines. Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp despite $\\kappa \\in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}.\n\n\\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\\pm$2\\,pp corresponds to ${\\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~multi-seed SC variability $\\sigma_{\\text{SC}} = 1.67$--$2.51$\\,pp on FOLIO, with $\\sigma_\\Delta$ up to 2.70\\,pp (Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}), placing $\\pm$2\\,pp within natural noise; (3)~the observed $|\\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\\,pp, so $\\pm$2\\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\\pm$2\\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\\pm$1\\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\\pm$3\\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (minimum detectable effect (MDE) $\\leq 2$\\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\\Delta| \\leq 4.41$\\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\\Delta = +0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds yield $\\Delta = +0.92 \\pm 1.14$\\,pp (n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Oracle controls confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}12\\%$ of this ceiling (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}; Appendix~\\ref{app:pw-oracle-subtype}).\n\n",

    "diagnostics": null

}
